# Promises: the product record for guide writers

Reconciled 2026-11-03 for C1. The table keeps journey steps and variants, capability statements and terms as stable primary records. Stories and acceptance scenarios cite their primary record rather than repeating it. Reader names follow guide/AGENTS.md. AI and agent items are held for the later writing wave; digests and workspace following are in scope now.

**Audit status: provisional.** The mechanical lead table is no longer a promise catalogue. Its release/package provenance has been attached to the journey records, and selected missing release rules have been written explicitly below. This does **not** certify that every clause of every release plan has been reconciled. In particular, attaching a release/package source to a journey is not a claim that every detail in that package is already represented by that journey. The remaining full-text review is listed below so later writers do not mistake extraction coverage for product completeness.

Source keys: UJ = docs/product-functionality/USER_JOURNEYS.md; CF = docs/product-functionality/CURRENT_FUNCTIONALITY.md; GL = docs/product-functionality/GLOSSARY.md; US = docs/product-functionality/USER_STORIES.md; AS = docs/product-functionality/ACCEPTANCE_SCENARIOS.md; CL = CHANGELOG.md; PL = the named docs/BASE_0_N_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md. A release-scoped source such as `0.8 D8` is distinct from `0.7 D8`.

Current records: 987; AI/agent records held for the later wave: 124. Added current release statements: R001–R054 (54).

## Superseded statements (do not write as current behavior)

- 0.6 WP4 per-record draft/activation/retirement: superseded by workspace change sets and publications (0.6 WP5–WP8, ADR-024/026/027; J05–J07).
- 0.5 single-process model administration restriction: superseded by 0.11 N1/N3; J12 covers several processes.
- 0.7/0.8 lower-case error spellings: superseded by 0.9 T1b; use upper-case codes.
- 0.8 D17 unsupported split topology: superseded by 0.11 N1/N3.
- 0.8 D35 mouse-only tree drag: superseded by 0.11 U1; J14.10 covers touch.
- 0.8 D41 5,000-record restructuring: superseded by 0.8 D44's 500-record limit; BEHAVIOUR-RULES B6.
- 0.9 C-D32 backslash-escaped parameter lists: superseded by C3b's repeated or comma-separated parameters with percent-encoded commas.
- 0.9 C-D96 separate webhook encryption key: superseded by 0.12's SECRETS_ENCRYPTION_KEY; run/secrets-and-keys.
- 0.9 C-D94/C-D110 acknowledgeLoss: superseded by reset; R028 and BEHAVIOUR-RULES J5.
- 0.9 D69 identical-only duplicate import rows: amended by 0.16 AP6's merging of complementary non-empty values; R032 and BEHAVIOUR-RULES I7.
- 0.10 D29 refusal to rename a key named by a consumer definition: superseded by 0.10 D54's reference rewriting; R035.
- 0.10/early 0.16 AI naming inference: removed in 0.16 AP6a; rules-based inference remains, R038.
- Early 0.16 assistant reading/matching/fixing phases and unpivot turns: superseded by the Claude Code session and AP10/AP13; later AI writing must use current task states.
- Boolean notification-mail choices: superseded by 0.16 AO3's now/digest/off delivery values; J57 and R039.
- 0.13's stopped documentation packages, 0.14's pending API-reference/palette/start-page proposals, 0.15's unbuilt interface translations and 0.17's workbook canvas are not current product promises.

## Reconciliation accounting

- Merged 41: explicit story/scenario cross-reference.
- Removed 1: test measurement.
- Removed 99: document heading, not a product statement.
- Removed 74: setting extraction replaced by restored setting catalogue in COVERAGE.
- Moved 605 released changelog leads to unreconciled evidence; removed 11 unreleased documentation-tooling leads.
- Removed 222: package heading, not a product statement.
- Removed 56: superseded or unbuilt plan lead.
- Moved 641 decision leads to unreconciled evidence; release-scoped replacement remains open.
- Removed 45: decision heading replaced by capability and rule sources.
- Removed 306: commit subject lead, not independent product evidence.

## Reading scope and remaining review

Read: guide/AGENTS.md, COMPLETE-COMMON.md, C1-RECORD.md; the existing promise/rule record and coverage inputs; release headings and extracted changelog bullets in chronological order; plan status, package headings and structured decision rows for 0.5–0.16. Full passages checked include the 0.3/0.5/0.6 release record, 0.7 fixes, the 0.16 replacement/removal record, AO3's decisions and cases, and the relevant 0.7–0.10 decisions cited by R001–R038. Code checks cover the live-create workspace requirement, webhook resume/reset, repeated-key import binding and digest preference refusals. Existing guide pages were indexed by text and heading; specific coverage claims require a matching passage, not merely an existing filename.

Not read exhaustively: the full prose of CHANGELOG.md's 0.8–0.12 release blocks and every delivered package/decision narrative of the 0.5–0.12 and 0.16 plans. Structured extraction is a navigation aid, not a substitute for that review. Those long files include historical implementations and successive amendments; their remaining clauses must be reconciled before C1 can be declared complete. No separate 0.3 or 0.4 implementation plan exists in docs/BASE_0_*_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md. The 0.13 stopped packages and the unbuilt 0.14/0.15 packages were excluded from current claims; 0.17 is outside this wave.

The removed mechanical leads remain recoverable from CHANGELOG.md and the source plans; their removal is not evidence that their details are all covered. COVERAGE.md is a conservative working ledger over the retained record and the other inventories, not a completion certificate for this remaining release audit.

## Unreconciled release evidence (not current promises)

These original leads are retained to prevent loss of unreviewed details. They can contain ellipses, obsolete behavior, delivery history and duplicate claims. Do not write from them until the complete cited passage and its later amendments have been read. Their coverage rows remain open; a topic match is not a verdict that a lead is current.

(3) Otherwise a new draft of the running release, named "The model of ‹workspace›". A remembered draft that is no longer current, because the workspace's model was changed elsewhere, gives up the link and stays in Settings › Model.. | PL-WM-D4 | Rule: **The API adds one route and one field.** `POST /api/v1/workspaces/{key}/model/draft` (action `model.author`; 200 with the draft found, 201 with a new one; `404 WORKSPACE_NOT_FOUND`, `409 WORKSPACE_RETIRED`). `workspaceKey` joins the draft and its outline. Apply stays the existing `installation`, then `activation` or `migrations`.. A client takes the same steps as the UI, with no combined route, like the load (D81). A concurrent first open waits on the workspace's row and gets the same draft. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan WM-D4 | source-review |
| PL-WM-D5 | Rule: **A ⋯ menu on the Workspace label's line**, at its right end, holds **Import…**, **Export…** and **Edit model**; Import and Export leave the collection page's header, which keeps its Add. The menu is `ui/ActionMenu` in `WorkspaceSelector`, the one component behind the rail on desktop and the band's workspace choice on a phone, with one workspace too. Each item follows its existing rule: Import where the data shown can be written and the person holds Bulk and import; Export where jobs run; Edit model with Model authoring and Model release in that workspace, not for a retired one. With no item the menu is not shown. Export keeps what the collection page shows (type, filters, search, parent, columns), which the page hands to the shell; on any other page it opens on the application's types. Manage workspaces stays where it is.. The user, 2026-10-31: these are not frequent actions and the page must not overwhelm. Cost: moving Import and Export changes existing tests and browser runners that press them, and PL6 (0.17), which reworks these two dialogs, star | steward | PL §0.16 plan WM-D5 | source-review |
| PL-WM-D6 | Rule: **The workspace mode is in the URL**: `/settings/model/drafts/{id}/editor?workspace={key}`, and the same parameter on the migration page. Without it the editor is as today, including for a draft that remembers a workspace.. Survives a reload and a shared link, and keeps "Opened from Settings › Model, the editor is as today". The journey parameter is not reused: it drives the journey bar. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan WM-D6 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D12 | Rule: **An assistant is a registered application, and a person signs in for it.** New: `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` and its `/mcp` form, the 401's `resource_metadata`, `POST /oauth/register` (RFC 7591), the table `agent_applications`, and `user_sessions.agent_application_id`. A public client with PKCE; `resource` must be this instance's `/mcp` when sent. Open registration (`MCP_OPEN_REGISTRATION`, default on) accepts loopback addresses and HTTPS hosts in `MCP_REDIRECT_HOSTS` (default none); anything else is registered by an administrator. Unused registrations are removed after 30 days. Client ID Metadata Documents are not built. **A session is bound:** one started for an application issues only `agent`, with its refresh token in the answer's body and rotated by the existing `sessions.renew`; a browser session never issues `agent`.. Registration is what Claude Code uses by default; a metadata document makes the engine fetch addresses an unknown caller names, a request-forgery risk and outbound traffic a closed deployment may not have. The session rows give si | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D12 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D13 | Rule: **The sign-in page is the consent.** When an application sent the person, the page says who asks (its own claimed name), where it returns (the host, which the specification requires shown), what it can and cannot do, and the buttons read "… and allow". Password, passkey, provider and second factor run unchanged.. Every sign-in route ends in issue_code; one banner in check_request's page needs no extra state or step. An open registration can claim any name, so the page says the name is claimed. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D13 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D14 | Rule: **`oauth_clients.kind`: `api` (existing) or `agent`.** An agent client gets only `scope=agent`, an API client never does. The AP8 session's client stays `api`.. An agent principal can then never hold a token that reaches an approve route (AG-D5), enforced at issue and not by each route. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D14 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D15 | Rule: **The `agent` role holds `browse` and `change-sets`; the same set is the ceiling** for every `agent` token, applied to the permissions read after the cache. A person needs no new grant: their own roles cut down to the ceiling. A client needs a grant as usual. `publish` and `approve` are not in it; consumer API grants pass unchanged.. AG-D3 as code, one definition. change-sets carries no capability until AG3 adds tools, and only the tools are reachable on this audience. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D15 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D16 | Rule: **Revises AG-D6. Each call is a row in a new `agent_calls` table, not an audit event:** who, application, tool, argument names (never values), outcome, duration, request ID; removed after `MCP_CALLS_DAYS` (90). **The audit `channel` column moves to AG3**, its first writer.. Today reads write no audit entry, and rows per read in audit_events would be permanent and sit in the trail that holds changes. ai_calls is the precedent; so is AP1 moving origin to its first writer, because nothing could write or | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D16 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D18 | Rule: **Two tools prove the registry:** `describe_access` (always: who I am, my ceiling, my workspaces) and `list_workspaces` (needs Browse somewhere, paged, the service function of `GET /workspaces`). A tool declares the group it needs; one the principal lacks is not listed and is refused if called.. The journey "lists only the tools its grants allow" cannot run with no tools. AG2 adds to the same registry. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D18 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D19 | Rule: **A definition's tool is offered where the caller holds its grant or Browse in the workspace.** The tool only: `/api/consumer` stays closed to anything but a `consumer` token with a grant (D306, D307 unchanged). Refines AG-D3's "the consumer definitions it is granted". A client holding only a consumer API grant sees that tool and `describe_access`, and no other tool.. The journey "what is the code of this value in the webshop system?" must work for a person. Grants narrow writes only (D243), so Browse already reads every record of the workspace, and a definition gives it a new shape and no new | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D19 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D20 | Rule: **Named by the definition, not by the workspace.** `lookup_<key>`, with a required `workspace` argument listing the workspaces the caller may read it in. Workspaces whose definitions are equal share one tool. Where a key differs between them, or the name would pass 48 characters, the name ends in a short hash of the definition. Hyphens stay; the characters allowed in a key are all legal in a name.. One tool per workspace would multiply the list for no gain when many workspaces run one model. 48 leaves room for Claude Code's prefix under Claude's 64. The list is read per caller, which _run already allows for. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D20 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D21 | Rule: **The check moves to `engine/reads/consumer_parameters.py`, taking the parsed names and values;** the route passes them from its request and behaves as before. A tool offers `objects`, `lists` and `xref`, those the definition offers, on live data, with `asOf`, its parameters and its `to.` targets. **Not offered:** `resolve` (a batch for systems; `xref` answers one lookup), `events`, `publications` and a pinned read. The tool's schema is flat with an `operation` argument, and what fits each operation is checked by the moved check.. One check, the same refusals. Claude's API refuses oneOf at the top of a tool's schema (from memory, to confirm in the real try), and the check exists anyway. An assistant asks about the current state; an integration that needs a | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D21 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D22 | Rule: **Eight curated tools join the two of AG1,** all needing Browse in the named workspace. The refusal is the same whether the workspace is missing or not readable: `describe_model` (applications, views, record types, relation types and consumer APIs, by key and name), `describe_type` (its attributes with their types and filters, parents, relationships, views), `find_records` (the `ModelQuery` of `POST …/queries/objects`), `read_record` (values, path, relationships), `mapping_coverage` (a view and `state`, `search`, `parentObjectId`; the binding is read from the view, as a route's caller must repeat it only to have it checked), `record_history` (`kind`, `actorId`), `list_changes` (`GET /changes`). Live data only: `changeSetId` joins them in AG3, whose tools read their own change set.. AG-D4's list, plus find_records and the two describe_*, without which an assistant cannot name what it asks about. Route bodies that hold their logic are split out as workspace_page was, so route and tool run one function. AO-D7's | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D22 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D23 | Rule: **`MCP_ANSWER_BYTES` (40,000).** A paged answer over it is read again with half the page size, which returns a cursor, never a truncated answer. One record over it is a refusal saying so. `pageSize` defaults to 50 on the new tools.. AG-D7. The SDK sends a dict twice (as structuredContent and as text, seen in lowlevel/server.py), and Claude Code refuses long answers (25,000 tokens by default, from memory, to confirm in the real try). The size of the first page | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D23 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D24 | Rule: **Resources:** `refiroo://workspaces/{key}/model` and its `/applications/{key}`, `/object-types/{key}` and `/views/{application}/{key}`, all listed for the caller's readable workspaces, a page at a time by the protocol's cursor. They are read through `_run`, so the limit and the log hold, recorded as `read_resource` with `uri` as the argument name. A resource answers whole, without the size cap.. The scope. Claude Code reads resources (its ListMcpResources and ReadMcpResource tools); an assistant that cannot has describe_model and describe_type, which call the same functions. A model's size is bounded by the model itself. | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D24 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D25 | Rule: **Every tool here says `readOnlyHint`,** and each generated tool's description ends by saying that values are data. AG-D8 stands as the server's instructions already say it. Answers keep the API's shapes, with no wrapper.. The hint lets an assistant treat a read as safe. A wrapper would make a tool's answer differ from its route's. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D25 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D26 | Rule: **Eleven tools, all needing Work in change sets in the workspace.** Reading: `my_change_sets` (open ones, with state and version), `read_change_set` (version, release, submission state, the last decision's comment), `change_set_changes` (rows, `kind` records or relationships, paged), `validate_change_set`. Writing: `create_change_set`, `add_record`, `change_record`, `set_field`, `add_relation`, `change_relation`, `submit_change_set`. Every write names `changeSetId` and `expectedChangeSetVersion`, and a change to a row its version too. **`changeSetId` joins `find_records`, `read_record` and `describe_type`** (AG-D22), which then read that change set and its pinned release. A write answers with IDs and the new versions.. The scope, with what an assistant needs to know that a person sent it back and why. describe_type must read the pinned release, or a change set on another release would be filled from the wrong model. Choosing a release needs mode | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D26 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D27 | Rule: **An assistant reads and writes only change sets its own principal created;** any other answers `CHANGE_SET_NOT_FOUND`. A person's own change set counts, so "add these to my change set" works. Narrower than the API, where anyone holding the group may write to any open change set.. AG-D8: text in a record could otherwise steer an assistant into a colleague's change set, under review by someone who did not ask for it. Cost: an assistant cannot help on a colleague's change set. Opening this later is additive. | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D27 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D28 | Rule: **A tool declares it writes.** `_run` then sets the writer (the ceiling's permissions, group `change-sets`, the tool's action, the principal), never allows withdrawal, and runs the tool once with no size check. Reads stay as they are. `validate_change_set` writes its audit entry once and cuts its own diagnostics to fit, with a cursor. `annotations` become per tool: writes and validation are not `readOnlyHint`, and no tool is `destructiveHint`.. The one writer the routes use gives the same grants, record-type limits (D243), audit actor and created_by. A retry after a lost answer cannot apply twice, as the version it names has moved. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D28 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D29 | Rule: **`origin = {kind: "agent", application, proposedBy}`, a type of its own beside `ProposalOrigin`.** `proposedBy` is the person or client's public ID, and `application` the assistant's name, copied as its row may be removed. `_run` sets it on the session, and `proposed.py` reads it before a task's, so AP-D32 and AP-D33 hold with no second place. Revises AG-D5's `{kind, client}`.. A person's browser edit and their assistant's edit must differ, so the principal cannot decide. AG-D13's "claimed" name stays claimed, so the mark says so. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D29 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D30 | Rule: **`audit_events.channel`, nullable, written `mcp` only,** set in `_name_actors` from the session, for every entry the ORM inserts during a call. Shown as `channel` on `GET /changes` and a record's history, additive. Null means not through an assistant; earlier rows stay null. No screen shows it, as the mark says it for records and relationships.. AG-D16 moved it here. The tools reach only ORM-built events, and one hook covers them. My proposal: no line on the Changes page, because a change-set event's actor already names a client, though for a person it does not say it cam | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D30 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D31 | Rule: **A refused tool call says `CODE: message`, then `details` as JSON,** on every tool, reads included. `_run` records it as now.. The scope, and an assistant needs currentVersion and diagnostics to correct itself. The refusal for a missing and an unreadable workspace stays identical. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D31 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D32 | Rule: **Left out on purpose:** removing a record, relation or value; clearing a field (`value` may not be null); withdrawing; discarding a change set; batches (`bulk` is outside the ceiling); restructuring.. AG-D3's "no deletion of anything", and the exit gate. A row added by mistake is corrected by a person, or the assistant starts a new change set. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D32 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D33 | Rule: **Two conditions on a relationship, and two stamps, so the list and the query mean the same.** `filter.related:<key>.under=<record ID>` keeps records with some related record under that ancestor record at any depth, `.notUnder=<record ID>` those with none; each such entry must hold. They are the query's hop with an `ancestor:<type>` criterion, as set statements in PostgreSQL (`list_relation_filters` builds one hop per type the related records can stand under, each starting from the ID of that type only, so a wrong type selects nothing), live and in a change set or publication, and `_related_predicate` now accepts the ancestor criterion on its hop. The query and an export say the same with `ancestor:<type>` and `not`. The filter bar's relationship editor gets "has none under" and "has any under" with a record picker over the types the related records can stand under (read from the served placements), and a pill reads "has none under Webshop". `createdAt` and `updatedAt` filter every live table list without being declared, offered in + Filter, and not in a change set or publication, which record no times (`UNRECORDED_TIMESTAMPS`). A field with two conditions, such as the two ends of a date range, is a pill for each. No domain key anywhere.. The journey cannot be said otherwise, and a capability only the question could reach would be a defect (the API is the product). The list, selection, bulk actions and export read the one filter, so what the person sees is what is | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D33 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D34 | Rule: **The answer is direct, not a job** (revises AP-D8 for this purpose). `POST /api/v1/workspaces/{key}/query-translations` takes `applicationKey`, `viewKey`, `question` (at most 500 characters), `changeSetId` or `publicationId`, `sendLevel`, and answers `filters`, `search`, `notExpressed` and `applied`. It needs Browse there and Use AI proposals (`ai_requested`). It reads the model, releases its transaction, asks through `calls.ask` and holds none while the provider answers (J48.8: J54.1 reads `pg_stat_activity` from inside the call), then checks the answer in a second transaction. Table views only: another type is `422 QUERY_TRANSLATION_VIEW_UNSUPPORTED`.. One short call with nothing to batch, cancel or resume; the person waits at the box. A job would add a worker round trip and an inbox notice for a second. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D34 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D35 | Rule: **Send level `structure`, and an offer rule of its own** (revised 2026-11-01: names of short lists at `samples` and `values`, see the revision below).** The provider is sent the question, today's date and, per field, its key, label, kind, conditions and, for a relationship, the names of the types it reaches and of the types they can stand under. No record name, value or ID. The UI offers Ask when a provider is set and the person holds ai-proposals in the workspace, at any send level (`queryTranslationOffer`, beside `aiOffer`); hidden, never disabled. It is not drawn on mapping grids, trees or Changes, which are not table views.. The question and a list's field names are the least anything can send, so every level allows it. The user's rule: hide what cannot be done. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D35 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D36 | Rule: **A record named in words is found by the engine, in the database, in the context asked about.** The model gives a name; the engine looks among the proper types (those the relationship reaches for "is any of", those its records can stand under for under and notUnder): the exact name, ignoring case, first; otherwise the one record whose name contains it. None, or several, is a part in `notExpressed` with the reason (up to five names), never a guess. A record created in a change set is found there and not live.. The model sees no record, so it cannot choose one. A wrong guess would change a list silently. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D36 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D37 | Rule: **Nothing is applied when any part is not expressed.** `applied` is false, the filters that could be built are still returned, and the client applies nothing: it shows the part, the proposed filters as dashed pills that do nothing and Use these filters. A sort is a part not expressed (the list's sort is a control of its own); so is a question with nothing to filter by.. The user's rule that a question the list cannot express is said so, not approximated. The person decides whether the rest is wanted. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D37 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D38 | Rule: **(Revised 2026-11-01: the fields are also every relationship of the type.) A field or condition the list does not offer, or a value its validation refuses, makes the whole answer a failed call.** The fields are the view's `filterFields` and, live, the two stamps. Each filter then goes through the list's own validation by building the list's statement with it; a refusal marks the call recorded as answered as `failed` (kind `malformed`), and the route answers `502 QUERY_TRANSLATION_FAILED` with `details.callId`, as it does for a provider that fails. Nothing comes back to apply, and the box keeps its words.. The model's answer is data to check, never a guess to repair (AP1). The UI says one sentence (errorMessage) and search still works. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D38 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D10 | Rule: **Two direct routes, no job.** `POST /api/v1/model-releases/{id}/impact-summary` and `POST /api/v1/model-drafts/{id}/impact-summary`, with `workspaceKey`, optional `sendLevel` and `language`; the release's route compares with the release the workspace runs, the draft's route also takes `expectedVersion`. An invalid draft is `422 IMPACT_SUMMARY_DRAFT_INVALID`. No transaction is held while the provider answers.. One short call with nothing to resume, as AG-D34. Revises AP-D8 for this purpose. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D10 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D11 | Rule: **The answer always carries every fact.** `facts: [{id, kind, text, path, count}]`, `statements: [{text, factIds}]`, `removedStatements`, `failure`, `cached`, `callId`. A provider that fails or answers out of shape is a `200` with `statements` empty and `failure` set, not a `502`.. AO-D9 asks for "facts alone" on a failure, so the route still has something true to give. Differs from AG4, where a failed call had nothing to show. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D11 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D12 | Rule: **A fact's text and ID come from the engine.** Kinds: `change` (one per diff entry), `data` (a compatibility diagnostic with its count), `contract` (a contract diagnostic), `blocked` (a diagnostic that refuses the activation) and `records` (records per touched type). The ID derives from kind, code and path, so the same fact keeps its ID. The text is built from the model's labels.. The reader checks the sentence against a line the AI did not write. count per touched type is one new GROUP BY over the workspace, whole set (AO-D3). / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D12 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D13 | Rule: **"Mapping changes" are relationship and view facts.** No separate kind, and saved model mappings stay out.. That is what a release can change, as found above. The sentence in the journey still reads as written, since a relationship's fact names its types. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D13 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D14 | Rule: **The provider gets at most 40 facts per kind, each with its true count; the person gets all.** The largest counts and blocked items come first; the rest is "and N more" in the context, and a statement may cite the group.. AO-D3. A release of another model can hold hundreds of changes. Text in before and after is cut at 200 characters in the context and sent as it stands in the model. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D14 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D15 | Rule: **The cache is a small table.** `ai_summaries`: `purpose`, `input_hash` (unique), `answer` JSON, `call_id`, `expires_at`, one hour on. The hash covers the facts, language, send level, provider, model and prompt version. A hit makes no call and returns `cached: true`. Expired rows are removed in one statement when a row is written. Migration: one table.. AO-D4's "same facts, same text, no second call" must hold across worker processes, which an in-process cache cannot. One hour is the preview's session in AO-D4. AO2 and AO3 reuse it. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D15 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D16 | Rule: **No new permission group.** AO-D5's "second action" would not be separately grantable, as grants are by group, so a holder of `model.activate` in the workspace who also holds Use AI proposals may ask. The group's description gains "and summaries". Hidden otherwise.. Grants are by group (ADR-044), so a second action changes nothing a person can see. The roles that hold Model release already hold the group. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D16 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D17 | Rule: **One component, only on request.** `ImpactSummary` in `features/model-admin`, in `ActivateDialog` and `ApplyDialog`, after the compatibility notice. Nothing is sent until the person presses **Explain this change**; the line beside it says what is sent. A new check clears the account.. A dialog opening must not spend money or send anything. One component, no copies. Facts appear only after asking, so with no provider the dialogs are as they are today (AP-D3: hidden). / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D17 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D18 | Rule: **The prompt says what the facts are and never judges.** It asks for short statements in the given language, each citing fact IDs, and forbids "safe", "risky" and advice. A check drops a statement with no valid ID (AO-D2). It does not scan words: the wording rule rests on the prompt, the citations and the note under the account.. Filtering words would give false comfort in any language. "Written by AI … It can be wrong" stays under the account. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D18 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D19 | Rule: **`language` is optional in the API and the UI sends none until 0.15 is built.** The default is English. A German account beside English facts is left for when 0.15 converts these dialogs.. Changes AO-D8's route to it, not its aim: an integrator can already ask for a language. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D19 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D21 | Rule: **Counts are of live data.** A record, relationship or field count reads only entries with a feed position. Work in an open change set appears only as that set's events (created, submitted, sent back, approved, discarded). The facts' heading says "live data".. Otherwise a published record counts twice (found above). Cost: edits still sitting in an open change set are not counted, only that the set exists and what happened to it. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D21 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D22 | Rule: **A publication says who prepared it.** A `source` fact for a publication reads its change set's staged entries in one grouped statement: the jobs and the people that prepared it, beside who published. `people` counts direct live changes and that preparation, as "changes".. The journey's "by 3 people, 190 in one bulk import" could otherwise almost never be said, as most bulk work reaches live data as a publication. Cost: one more grouped read over the workspace's entries, timed in the basics. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D22 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D23 | Rule: **Names go out only at `values`.** At `structure` the provider gets counts and the model's labels, and no name of a record, person, change set or file. The reader's fact lines always show them, as Activity does.. Those names are free text and personal data. Narrows AO-D1, which lists "actor" among what is sent. Cost: at structure an account says "one change set was sent back" and the citation names it. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D23 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D24 | Rule: **Two direct routes, no job.** `POST /api/v1/workspaces/{key}/history/account` and `POST /api/v1/objects/{objectTypeKey}/{id}/history/account`, with `since`, `until` (included, not included, as `/changes`), optional `timeZone` (for "on Tuesday"; UTC by default), `sendLevel`, `language`. No period limit. `422 HISTORY_PERIOD_INVALID` for an empty or reversed period or an unknown time zone. Needs Browse and Use AI proposals in the workspace; hidden otherwise.. Revises AO-D7's "job when the period is large", on the measure above, and as AO-D10. If the basics show otherwise it goes in the record, with the figures. A record's account reads through audit_event_subjects. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D24 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D27 | Rule: **Citation to fact, fact to the changes.** A fact carries a `link`: a record, a change set, a publication, a job, or a narrowing of the list. **Show them** narrows Activity in place under `ScopeBanner`, or filters a record's timeline by kind and person. `GET /changes` gains `objectTypeKey`, `causeId` and `live=true`, whole set, additive. A removed job has no link and says "a job, since removed".. The journey asks for links to the records. live is needed so the narrowed list is what the fact counted. Cost: one more filter set, on the JSON subject, within the workspace's rows. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D27 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D28 | Rule: **The facts through the AG2 tools, not the account:** a `history_facts` tool returns the engine's facts for a workspace or a record over a period, with no provider call, and needs Browse only. *(Revised 2026-11-02, approved by the user: the first wording, a `history_account` tool, could never be offered, as an assistant's token is cut to Browse and Work in change sets, AG-D15.)*. AO-D7. The assistant words the facts itself; the agent ceiling is unchanged. Cost: one tool, and no provider account through an assistant. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D28 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D29 | Rule: **Only on request.** The section sits above the Activity filters and on the record's History panel. Nothing is sent until **Explain this period**; the line beside it says what goes, at the level that applies. Default period: 7 days for a workspace, 30 for a record. An empty period makes no call and says so.. As AO-D17. Cost: without a provider the facts are not shown either (AP-D3), as in AO1. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D29 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D31 | Rule: **The digest keeps D432: kinds, counts and links, no names.** A line names the kind in its label's words; a job's line adds its job kind and counts (rows applied, rejected) read from the job while it exists. No change set, file, workspace or person name.. The rule protects mail that travels and sits in mailboxes, and a digest is the same mail, only fewer. | PL-AO-D33 | Rule: **Each kind is mailed at once, in the digest, or not at all;** one digest frequency per person, daily or weekly. | PL-AO-D34 | Rule: **Daily at 07:00 in the person's time zone, weekly on Monday at 07:00.** The page sends the browser's time zone when the person chooses a digest; the API takes `timeZone` (IANA, `422 UNKNOWN_TIME_ZONE` otherwise) and answers the next send time. | PL-AO-D35 | Rule: **Queued in the mail sender's round, beside `queue_notices`, one person per transaction.** `queue_digests` takes people whose `notice_digest_due_at` has passed, `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, gathers their unread, unhandled notices of digest kinds made since `notice_mail_since` in one statement (counts grouped in PostgreSQL), queues one message, sets those notices' `mailed_at` and moves `notice_digest_due_at` to the next send time, all in one commit. Nothing to say: no mail, the time still moves on. Mail not set up: nothing is queued and nothing moves. `queue_notices` skips digest kinds.. The plan's "one digest per person per period, no duplicates across nodes" comes from the row lock and the moved due time, as the notice mail already does it; no new worker, lease or schedule. | PL-AO-D38 | Rule: **Decided by the user 2026-11-02 (the recommendation accepted).** **A publication reaches those who follow the workspace.** A person follows workspaces they may Browse, off by default. **Where (the user, 2026-11-02): on the home page,** a "Follow" toggle on each workspace card (the cards are the workspaces the person may Browse, `GET /me/workspaces`, which gains `following`); Settings › Notifications lists the followed workspaces with "Stop following". Not on the workspace's Activity, which sits under Settings › Administration › Workspaces and which a reader may never reach. A change set published in a followed workspace, from an approved request or directly, puts a notice of the new kind `change-set.published` ("A change set was published in a workspace you follow") in each follower's inbox, but not the publisher's, in the publication's transaction, one `INSERT … SELECT` over the followers who still hold Browse there. From there it is a kind like the others: at once, in the digest or no mail (AO-D33), its line without names (AO-D31). Publications only, not withdrawals. People only: clients have the webhooks and the change feed. API: `GET /me/followed-workspaces`, `PUT` and `DELETE /me/followed-workspaces/{workspaceKey}` (`404` for a workspace the person cannot Browse, repeat harmless). Migration: `workspace_followers` (`user_id`, `workspace_key`, `created_at`; both keys cascade).. Every other kind is about something that needs the person or is theirs; a publication is others' work, and the inbox keeps every notice (D434), so sending it to every reader would fill each reader's inbox with no way to stop it. F | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D38 | source-review |
